# THE FP COHERENCE FOLD — one read of the whole corpus, the laws it was missing, the conflicts between its volumes ruled, and every volume amended in place

## Chair session 2fc13d59 (Fable 5.1), 2026-10-16. The owner's word: "read them
## comprehensively, refine them, and then can you refine them as a whole to make
## them completely cohesive and coherent?" + "complete with adversarial review".
## Thirteen reader seats read every FP volume WHOLE (48,891 lines across thirty
## files: the spine, the couplings, thirteen program design/architecture pairs,
## the compiled fold, the four later volumes CN/LG/PG/MF, and the war-rulings,
## realm-directives and route-lifecycle references), each against the code of
## record `origin/master` @ `00b648c9d` and the build record (the FP progress
## board, FP-ORDER, the ledger by grep). Their findings — a status table, the
## laws verbatim, every cross-program claim graded, internal contradictions,
## stale premises, the clock and tier of every mechanism, a felt-value verdict
## per mechanism, the vocabulary, the in-place refinements, and the owner's
## questions — are the kit `settlement-engine-kits/fp-coherence-2fc13d59-2026-10-16/findings/`
## (SP-CW · GR · IN-ES · TR-EC · WF · POP · INT · WY · HB · WC-1 · WC-2 · EP ·
## FOLD-SMALL), ~5,000 lines of receipts. THIS VOLUME is the chair's fold of
## them: it rules what a chair may rule, routes what is the owner's, and
## applies every in-place correction to the volumes (§8) so that no law is
## re-narrated and no stale sentence stands unbracketed.

**Status: THE FOLD IS LAW WHERE IT RULES AND A ROUTING WHERE IT ROUTES.** Its
chair rulings are vetoable by one owner clause; its owner rows (§7) decide
nothing on silence. Nothing here schedules a build: the owner's standing
order (UI + prose release first; "everything else after"; the EC dispatch gate
"pushed and deployed") binds. The in-place amendments (§8) are dated brackets
beside the text they correct — never a rewrite of a law, a ruling or an owner
quotation.

**Evidence class.** Every substrate statement is a READ at `00b648c9d`
(CONFIRMED in the brief's sense); under the estate's convention nothing here
is executed evidence. Where a reader reasoned rather than read, the findings
file says PLAUSIBLE and this fold carries the word.

**Base for amendment.** The kit holds two copies of each volume: the ledger
branch's (`volumes/`) and master's (`volumes-master/`). Master's carry later
corrections (the spine's alignment erratum; the compiled fold's §12 Edit Mode
and §13 Seam family; small line refreshes in ES, IN, TR, GR, WC). THE BASE IS
MASTER where a master copy exists; the ledger branch receives the same
amended text (the two copies are one truth after this fold). CN, MF, PG and
EC exist only on the ledger branch and are amended there (LG is on master too, S4 m3).

---

## §0 READING ORDER AND THE SHAPE OF WHAT WAS FOUND

Read: this volume §1 (the constitutional amendment) → §2 (the status truth
table — replaces every volume's header as the state of record) → §3 (the
cross-program conflicts, ruled) → §4 (the clock) → §5 (the vocabulary
register) → §6 (the felt core) → §7 (the owner rows) → §8 (what was amended
where) → §9 (the adversarial review). Then the spine, then any family.

**The corpus is three things, not one.** A LIT CORE (pact formation and its
compliance, the faith unseating ladder, believed scarcity and conditions, the
seat's errand spine built dark, the cascade braid built dark, the causal
headline built dark); a BUILT-BUT-DARK MIDDLE (information and espionage almost
entire, TR-1..3, the commons voice, habit's substrate, WY-1); and an UNBUILT
RIM (populations entire, interior entire, wayfare beyond its first wave, faith
beyond the unseating, TR-4..8, war circulation, the epoch, EC). No volume's
header tells the truth about which third it is in; fourteen say "nothing
scheduled, every wave dark" and two of those families light in the default
preset.

**The worst findings are mechanisms whose felt sentence cannot fire**, not
stale line numbers: the lure four families count on cannot be commissioned
(no caller for `lureCommission`); faith pacts cannot cross in any shipped
preset (`believedDevotionEnabled` is lit nowhere); the migration menu is
empty in every shipped world (nothing writes the route network), and lighting
routes beside demographics would cut every settlement's food imports to a
quarter with no sentence naming why; `mutual_defense` is wired to nothing;
the two-sided negotiation the spine calls settled law was never built (the
responder values alone); GRAMMAR lit without its own done-gate ever started;
HB's flagship, the owner's own example sentence, is not expressible at the
war chooser; the W-OPS design governs espionage from outside every volume.

**Three structural problems sit above every family and are this fold's real
job:** the clock (about a dozen modules read the one interval table, about
sixty constants in forty-five modules are hand-keyed, two wrong by their own
comment, and the walker the constitution promised was never built); the
ownership gaps between programs (the trade executors claimed by two programs
and built by neither, a commons grievance composite with five programs adding
terms and no owner of its budget, a coupling registry that minted pair ids the
design never had); and the constitution's own silences (no law ties a
mechanism's clock to how far a DM advances, none requires a wave to declare
what world it needs, none requires a constant to come from evidence, none
requires a reported erratum to be applied).

---

systematically failed (the spine reader's §12, confirmed by every family read).

15. **THE BINDING-SCOPE LAW.** Every FP volume, present and future — the
    thirteen the compiled fold names, the later programs (EC, MF, LG, PG, CN,
    W-SEAT, W-OPS, W-COIN, W-FAITH where they touch an FP seam) and their
    successors — is bound by requirements 1–24. A volume that declares itself
    outside says so in its header with the reason. (Today the spine binds "the
    six program volumes and the coupling volume" by name; nine later volumes
    cite the spine zero times.)
16. **THE FIRST-VISIBLE-LATENCY LAW** [CLARIFIED 2026-10-17 by the owner, verbatim: "Remember that we are building something that is mostly going to be behind the scenes in regard to the simulator. The DM wants emergent experience that only when scrutinized upon shows coherence. Not the other way around we’re not trying to show the full machinery." — so every 'first-visible sign' and 'felt sentence' is an OUTCOME in the world's voice (what happened to whom), never the machinery (bands, weights, odds, holds, mechanisms); the WHY is available on SCRUTINY (the Why panel, the causal walk, receipts) when the DM asks, never pushed onto the surface. Three layers: SURFACE (emergent outcomes) · SCRUTINY (coherence on demand) · MACHINERY (never shown).] [REFINED 2026-10-17 by the owner, verbatim: "Caravan should be shown on the map and condition words yes. But as toggles on the Caravan piece. What I’m trying to say is is that things that can build immersion should be shown. Things that are just very complex should not be unless viewed upon intentionally." — the test is IMMERSION versus COMPLEXITY: what builds immersion IS shown, behind toggles (caravans on the map as a layer; condition words on the caravan's own piece; menaces, borders and the like); what is merely complex is shown ONLY when the DM looks on purpose (the drill-down). The machinery is not forbidden from view; it is never pushed.] [RESTATED 2026-10-17 by the owner, verbatim: "Well, the point is to produce outcomes at the DM can’t predict (that’s the point for the whole simulator, emulating dwarf fortress), but still remains completely legible and coherence when scrutinized" — UNPREDICTABILITY IS THE GOAL, never a defect: an outcome the DM could not have foreseen is the product working (the Dwarf Fortress standard); the test is the other half, that every outcome is COMPLETELY legible and coherent when scrutinized (every link named, every change carrying its reason, no runaway that reads as a bug). Reviews never cite surprise as a risk; they cite illegibility, incoherence and runaways. The owner, the same minute, verbatim: "That’s also why there’s undo button and that the dungeon master still has control over everything" — surprise is SAFE because the DM can undo any advance, edit any state (requirement 14) and decide any hearing (requirement 24); the engine is never made tamer to protect the DM's story.] ⛔ RULED BY DELEGATION 2026-10-16, OD-FC-1 (the spine reader
    rightly says a product rule about what a DM must see is the owner's).
    Proposed text: every mechanism declares, beside its clock, its FIRST-VISIBLE
    LATENCY in DM advances — the DM's advance unit is week / month / season /
    year — and a one-month advance must surface at least the mechanism's
    fast-layer sign (a band word, a beat, a dossier line); the slow verdict is
    named separately. A mechanism whose first-visible latency is a year or
    more is built only behind one that is felt in a month, or behind the epoch
    program. A wave whose FIRST-VISIBLE line fails its experience read does not
    LIGHT: its lighting unit is refused until the sign fires. (The four experience reads of 10-02 found the lit layers "nearly
    silent at the table"; the family reads found most felt drama at the
    seasonal or yearly clock.)
    [AMENDED 2026-10-17 (fp-final; source `briefs/CHAIR-NOTES-FOR-THE-FOLD.md` item 5; §7 OD-FC-55; by delegation, vetoable) — THE SLOW SYSTEMS' SIGNS: this requirement binds every SLOW mechanism without exception — the week the crown's hold moves, a line says so (PL-2); the season a rite spreads, the dossier says so (PL-11's planting line, §4); the slow verdict names its planting. The fast actors are the couplings that bring slow systems to the table (a kingdom-wide ban met at the party's gate, `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_CH.md` §11; a saint walking in, CH-3) — the reason chains C13 THE PARTY and C12 THE CHAMPIONS sit early in §6's order (THE ORDER NOTE). The age receipt (chain C8; OD-FC-51) LEADS with the slow systems: the crown, the faiths, the economy's drift.]

    EC-16/18, runs only under spatial canon with a non-omniscient infoMode;
    faith runs only on deity-bearing premium worlds; no volume says so at the
    top.)

The WORLD CANON is geography, a world fact, not the simulator. A TIER line's "spatial canon" names the world's geography tier, never an entitlement; on a map-module world the canon is born with the campaign, so there the spatial arm is the default arm and the aspatial arm the degraded one. MEASURED at master `81f1cd963` (CONFIRMED by read): the one explicit canon entitlement today is the canonize call's own (`campaignWorldPulseSlice.js :: canonizeCampaignWorldSpatial`: `if (state.auth?.tier !== 'premium') return { ok: false, reason: 'not_entitled' };`), and only a premium or staff account can create a campaign (`campaignSlice.js :: createCampaign`: `const canCreate = current.auth?.tier === 'premium' || staffUnlocksPaidFeatures(current.auth?.role);`) — so H22's birth canon crosses no paying boundary that exists today; it reaches staff, and any lower tier the owner later admits to campaigns, as geography only. What H22 does move is BEHAVIOUR: a canon at birth lights every canon-gated lane from week one (the map document's own "behaviour switch"; its population-9.3-1 and plague-10.2-3), a declared new-world shift carried to the owner in §7 OD-FC-62 beside the born campaign's switches. The spine's requirement 17 takes the same bracket.]

22. **THE ENGINE-STATE LIFECYCLE AND ERRATUM LAW.** (a) ARCH_SP's L4
    lifecycle-paths clause (create / read / persist / regenerate / undo /
    import / migrate / dark-after-lit / death / veil) binds every engine-written
    state, not only DM edits (requirement 14b); (b) an erratum reported against
    the constitution or a volume lands as a dated bracket in the same commit as
    the ruling that makes it true — "a bug to report" is not a disposition
    (three constitution errata stood unapplied for ten weeks: the alignment
    read, the errand ledger's home, the posture names); (c) requirement 11
    points at the owner's EVIDENCE LAW (2026-10-02/03, "complete, not cryptic";
    pillar 10) and the voice program ("actions, not potential") as binding on
    every phrased kind — COUPLINGS §9's contract sentences and §8 of every
    family are re-authored at build under it.
23. **THE RETENTION-HORIZON LAW.** Every ring (pulseHistory 80, wizardNews
    240, turningPoints 24, the provenance edge cap) declares its horizon in
    INTERVAL_WEEKS at a measured event rate, and every advance unit declares
    what it evicts: a `one_year` advance appends 52 records into the 80-ring
    before collapsing to one, so the ring nets to 29 (findings EP §6 S1, read
    from `historyRingCollapse.test.js:150-159`) — a year-cadence DM's receipt
    and replay horizon is about 29 advances, not 80, and requirement 7's cause
    walk ages out 2.7× faster at that clock. The ring policy itself (collapse
    before eviction, or raise the cap) is RULED BY DELEGATION 2026-10-16, OD-FC-33.
24. **THE HEARING WEEK** (the owner, 2026-10-16, 18:5x EDT — the file's own write stamp, S1-12 — verbatim: "When it
    comes to conflict, spying, treatise, etc, anything that involves one
    settlement with another, the height of that engagement, regardless of where
    it is at, is paused at a week so that the user can always fix them however
    they want. … two antagonistic armies meet in a road. Their result is not
    instant and scrapped as part of advance time in the same week where the DM
    can't do anything. Instead, they pause at the 'start of battle' and the DM
    is given choice to optionally weight in on who is to win. Same with NPCs in
    other settlements or with other settlement's NPCs for any act of foreign
    policy. An envoy must stay a week in a settlement before the decision is
    made. A spy must stay a week as well, and anything related." — and the
    three clarifications: "this only applies to week by week advancements.
    months seasons and years go by automatically." · "The simulator is gated
    for premium" · "some options aren't just settled in a week. For example a
    siege. a siege takes a long time and either side may get allies to turn the
    tide over the course of weeks. still the DM can decide who wins during any
    one of those weeks forcing the hand.")
    (a) Every ENGAGEMENT between two settlements — an act of one court, army,
    envoy, spy, caravan, column, legate, house, or any NPC of one settlement
    upon another settlement or its NPCs — is a row in ONE ENGAGEMENT REGISTRY,
    seeded from the census of every live inter-settlement act (§3.4) and CLOSED
    once lit (an unregistered kind may not resolve; registration is the only
    door) (kind · owning program · its CLIMAX tick · its
    verdict vocabulary · whether it is a CLIMAX kind, settled in one hearing, or
    a STANDING kind that holds for weeks). [EXTENDED 2026-10-17 by OD-MN-8
    (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_MN.md` §11, by delegation) — an engagement between a
    settlement (or its army) and a MENACE, the DM-named outside force, is also
    a registered kind (`menace_strikes` STANDING, `menace_met` CLIMAX); a
    menace's raiding is its stressor's ordinary life, not a hearing.] (b) [RULED 2026-10-16 after skeptics
    S1-01 and S5-B1 — THE DOCKET EXISTS BUT THE PAUSE CURSOR CANNOT HOST A
    HEARING: the landed majors docket (`decisionTier.js`, eleven majors;
    `advanceInterval.js:601`; J-D7's "ONE gathered adjudication screen" and
    "durable HELD DOCKET") is the SCREEN and the ledger idiom SP-9 generalises,
    but its paused cursor re-runs the same parked tick and refuses advances and
    edits while parked (`advanceInterval.js:327-340`,
    `campaignWorldPulseSlice.js:450-459`, `settlementPendingEdits.js:136-142`),
    so a hearing NEVER parks the cursor.] At a one-week advance — the DM's
    chosen unit `one_week`, not a tick labelled `one_week` inside a longer
    advance — the engine never resolves a climax in the tick it arrives: the
    resolver writes the engagement to the HELD DOCKET (a flag-gated ledger) with
    its default verdict, computed deterministically with its epoch, and the tick
    COMPLETES normally; the world is in its ordinary between-advance state, so
    the DM may advance, edit or seal; the DM may leave the hearing to the engine,
    TILT it (a bounded thumb for one side, slight · heavy — a declared bounded
    term on the decider's score where the decider draws no randomness, a draw
    bias where it does; each kind declares which) or give a VERDICT (the forced
    outcome; the word "decree" is the landed Decree Registry's and is not used);
    the NEXT advance resolves every docketed hearing FIRST, from the seal or the
    default, before anything else moves. A STANDING engagement (a siege, a
    blockade, an embargo, an occupation's contest, a long negotiation, a spy's
    stay) offers the same three choices EVERY week it stands, and the verdict
    forces the hand that week. The HEARING SEAL is its own Edit Mode seal kind,
    keyed by ENGAGEMENT id with the registry's verdict vocabulary, read by the
    resolver FIRST (before any feasibility, max-age or capitulation arm) in the
    resolver's own module — it is not a fork pin, so the pinnable-fork list in
    the frozen `pulseKernel.js:362` is untouched (S5-B2); the STRESSOR PIN of
    ODQ §128 is a different object (it holds a stressor; the hearing seal decides
    a week's verdict) and both stand.
    week. (c) A month, season or year advance runs every HEARING on the engine's
    default without stopping — the owner's "months seasons and years go by
    automatically" governs hearings only; the landed majors pause and its DM
    switch (`advanceAutoResolve`, realm directive 7) are UNTOUCHED (S5-B3: that
    pause is the DM's option, and its majors include acts that are not between
    two settlements); each hearing still mints its docket record for one tick and
    leaves its receipt ("resolved by the engine's default verdict"), so the cause
    walk and the Herald see the same world either way. When the flag is dark or
    the world regenerates, docketed hearings are not read: a dark world resolves
    every climax in its tick as today, and a regeneration re-derives the docket
    from the world (it is derived state, dropped and re-minted, requirement 22a).
    (d) The seal is a
    typed DM edit (requirement 14; L10's one door) whose seal EVENT is minted
    with its own id and named as the verdict's parent in `causedBy` (the field
    holds parent ids — S5), replayed exactly as an order in the world (EP B2's
    reading), surviving every lifecycle path (requirement 22a). (e) The
    simulator is PREMIUM: the hearing, the tilt and the decree are premium
    surfaces (U16's gate), with no free-tier arm to design. (f) Dark-identical
    behind `hearingWeekEnabled`; lit, every climax resolves one tick later than
    today — a declared same-seed shift signed once at lighting. (g) Every
    engagement kind's pending card is its FIRST-VISIBLE sign (requirement 16)
    and is written under the Evidence Law: who, upon whom, where, the default
    verdict, and nothing about what the DM might do; at a month-or-longer
    advance the RECEIPT ("resolved by the engine's default verdict") is the sign.

| 3.3.15 | **THE BANNER — patrols in a kingdom** [ADDED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4; `briefs/RULINGS-ROUND3.md` §F.1–F.7; `briefs/RULINGS-ROUND4.md` A.1, A.7 and §E (PL-r4-S1 · STOP-PR-r4-1, RE-RULED); SEAMS-r3-6's fold half; `cure/D-PR-r4.md`, `cure/D-PL-r4.md` and `cure/D-PS-r4.md`, routed to the fold); the owner's word of 2026-10-18 ~19:2x EDT, verbatim on the BOARD: "Effectively, every patrol in a kingdom is working under the highest overlord banner, and every subsequent level until it reaches them inteh vassal chain with a home local font thet is their home settlement that they more or less take charge in and can with some flexibility operate in unity to cover the entirety of the kingdom region. This si also how we can express protection from the crown. so it blurs the line a bit between territories."] — three volumes held three readings of a patrol beyond its own land (PR: the ally ladder at every hold; PS: "members stand as allies"; PL: no rider row) | RULED (§F, by delegation, vetoable), now in the volumes: ONE HOME — the settlement that raises, pays and houses a patrol has first call on its own roads; its LOYALTY STACK is PL's chain read rung by rung (`polityOf`'s path with `crownReachAt` at each rung, cut at a suspended link), DERIVED and NEVER STORED (PR §10.3 (a); PL §4.7 F.1). ONE SHARE LAW — the liege's share of a member's riders is the same monotone band as its share of the member's force and decisions, never a second share (PL §4.7 F.2). THE LINE BLURS BY THE HOLD — the crown's net is called on the crown's OWN read of its hold, and each crossing's right on the HOLDER's own belief (THE PASSAGE's read): under a hold the holder believes `staunch` or `unbroken`, a same-kingdom patrol rides a member's roads BY RIGHT OF THE BANNER (THE PASSAGE's permit source `banner`, no tolerance spent; OD-PS-45) and coverage composes across members' borders under the one per-stretch cap; under a weaker hold each lord keeps his roads and a neighbour-lord's riders take the ally size tolerance; at a break the crown's riders are a FOREIGN UNIT in a rebel's land, under THE PASSAGE's rules (PR §10.3; PS §5.5, §14). PROTECTION IS A DEED — riders PLACED on a levied member's stretches are the fealty record's `protection_kept`, none placed `protection_failed`; a lender's credit is ONE `service_answered` row a year on PL's own episode `riders_lent:<rootId>:<yearStartTick>` (PL §4.7 F.4, F.6). THE TWO RE-RULINGS (§E): (1) PL-r4-S1 — on an incursion the OUTCOME is the crossing's HARM whatever the answer was (`protection_kept` when the member suffered no incursion harm, `protection_failed` when it did) and the ANSWER moves trust, so A.6's "passed-through after `kept` = `protection_failed`" is WITHDRAWN; (2) STOP-PR-r4-1 — PL §4.1 STANDS (under `unbroken` the crown may summon every member DIRECTLY), and §F.1's "never skipping a lord" reads "never skipping a lord's ROW": the mid-lord's row is written either way (a credit when it lets its sub-vassal's riders go; a resentment patch when the crown rode past it unasked); under every weaker hold the call goes rung by rung. NO NEW REGISTRY KIND: the banner rides `call_in_answer`'s one input line and THE PASSAGE's existing cards; `banner` is a prose word and one permit-source value (§5 ↳ r4). PRICE: `gradeLegMultiplier` / `hopOf` price mode `way` at `HIDDEN_PATH_SLOWDOWN` (2) at every rung and flag it hidden; beasts × 1 by their own law; BD's `TRAIL_WEEKS_MULT` struck (RULINGS-ROUND4 §E). USE: `useCount` on the way-mode edge, decayed on the lifecycle's clock — never a flow class. JUNCTIONS: a way-mode edge may end at a junction (a point on ONE primary hop, the map's ask); a card's WHERE stays `road (a, b)` keyed `junction:<id>` (§3.4 WHERE ↳ r5); THE BODIES' derived bypass key `junction:<a\|b>@<b>` stands until the map names its junctions. KNOWLEDGE is separate from status (the contract): `knownTrailsOf` (§3.3.16 ↳ P4 (9)). **The one lit chain** (restated per preset after skeptic S1-05's executed census): in the DEFAULT preset `realistic_regional` a DM feels a pact proposed, valued by the responder, signed or declined, kept or frayed; and a patron's fall — which is VACUOUS on every born world because the birth preset seats no deity (FAITH:10, FPQ-62). Renewal, conversion, succession at an heir and mediation are lit only in `dramatic_campaign` and `full_simulation`; believed scarcity and conditions colour pacts only in the three belief presets, never the default. Everything else in thirty volumes is a flag away or a build away.

**THE WHERE OF EVERY KIND** [ADDED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r1; HOST-r1-6; the BOARD's host ruling (3) of 2026-10-18 00:41; the map's ENC-HL-13: "Every engagement kind needs a place the map can name", and H16's border segment as a hearing card's "where")] — insertions only, so the registry's place column is this companion table; each owning volume carries the same cell in its own registry (PL, MN and CH did so in round 1). The closed list is `ENGAGEMENT_WHERE_KINDS` = `settlement · road · country · site · border_segment` (the identifier FREE on master; the values reuse landed words in their place meanings — `road` a registered homonym of the route grade). `site` is ADDITIVE when THE SITES' record lands (§3.1.28); `border_segment` is OFFERED only — the map's reading through H16, lit on the owner's word, never a host fact before it.

|---|---|---|

The CHAIR's rulings on the registry: (1) the registry and the PENDING LEDGER are built by ONE spine wave, **SP-9 THE HEARING WEEK** (the ledger shape, the HEARING SEAL kind (keyed by engagement id; engine · tilt · verdict), J-D7's adjudication screen as the pending panel, the held docket read-first at the next advance, the premium gate, the flag `hearingWeekEnabled`, the per-kind `registerEngagementKind` with a closure pin that grows only by registration), dark; (2) the first rows LIT are the kinds already alive: `pact_acceptance`, `envoy_hearing`, `siege` (seal 6 generalised), `armies_meet` (the live encounter); every other row lands with the wave that builds its kind, as an obligation of that wave's charter (bracketed in §8's volumes); (3) once lit, a kind outside the registry cannot resolve — the resolver's split (docket-then-resolve) is enforced by a walker over every REGISTERED resolver, seeded from §3.4's census of live acts so it is green on day one (S5-B4); (4) SP-9 is THE HEAD OF THE CHAIN ORDER (§6): every later felt chain is felt through its hearings; (5) the pulseKernel stays frozen — the split lives in each resolver's own module, the ledger write in `spatialLedgers`, no pause cursor at all (S5-B1): the tick completes and the next advance resolves the docket first; J-D7's adjudication screen is the panel; the majors pause stays the DM's option (directive 7).

The same word stands in SP-9's charter line in `DESIGN_FP_ARCH_SP.md` ("The four live kinds register first: `pact_acceptance`, `envoy_hearing`, `siege`, `armies_meet`") — owed by an insertion-only bracket there (no P5c lane owns that volume; routed, `cure/D-FOLD-p5.md`) — and in GR's registration bracket (`DESIGN_FP_ARCH_GR.md`, D-BRACKETS-p5); `DESIGN_FP_SPINE.md` carries no `envoy_hearing` (0 hits).]

The column is the registry's: SP-9's card shape carries it and every owning volume's row inherits it without a cell of its own; `participation` is a prose word in 20 master `src` files with 0 quoted literals (`cure/D-CH-p5-probe/census-p5.out`) — a card field, registered in §5 (P5).]

## §4 THE CLOCK — one law, one measured defect class, one walker, one owner row

**The law** is requirement 16 (§1, OD-FC-1 for the owner's word on the product rule). Its evidence, from the family reads (§7 of each findings file): drama lives at the seasonal or yearly clock in every family — SP-C's posture (156 weeks), credibility's 52-week half-life, a spy's two-year expected life at `EXPOSE_BASE` 0.01, INT-5 and WC-15 at the epoch, R6's release standing on the occupation ladder, every war-close fold — while a DM advances weeks between sessions. The fast-layer sign each mechanism must declare is named per family in §8's brackets ("FIRST-VISIBLE: …").

**The measured defect class** (findings WY §7, CONFIRMED by read):

| Class | What | Count | Cure |
|---|---|---|---|
| A — the 12-tick year | constants that assume a 12-tick year: `STARVE_FULL_TICKS = 12` ("a season"), `CREDIT_TERM: 12` ("a season or two"), ten unlabeled 12s | 12 | the two comment-mismatch values are same-seed shifts: OD-FC-15. The ten unlabeled 12s are measured one by one by the walker's first run; each is a cure row or an exemption with a reason |
| B — shadow clocks | local re-declarations of the interval grid (`WEEKS_PER_YEAR`, `SEASON_WEEKS = 13`, `tick / 52`, the kernel's inline table, two same-named `INTERVAL_WEEKS` shadows) | ~20 modules | each becomes an `INTERVAL_WEEKS` read where the file is not frozen; the frozen kernel's inline table is an exemption WITH the frozen-file reason |
| C — authored durations as bare literals | 13/26/52 hand-keyed in ~45 FP and world kernels (right values, underived) | ~60 constants | the walker ACCEPTS them when the file imports `INTERVAL_WEEKS` and the literal is a named constant whose comment names the season/half-year/year; it REDS a bare literal in an expression |

**The walker** (CHAIR ruling, vetoable): requirement 9 already promised "a time-base walker"; building a promised instrument is REPAIR, not new capability. SP-7 (now freed of the mode table, §3.1.11) is re-chartered as `tests/lint/timeBase.walker.test.js`: a shrink-only exemption list seeded from the Class A/B/C census above, a RED on any new bare 12/13/26/52 in `src/domain` outside the list, zero behaviour. No constant moves in the walker's commit. It rides the next spine unit. The mode-speed table stays WY-1's.

**What a DM advance IS** (for the latency law): the `RealmForecast` advance units are week / month / season / year; the engine composes a year as 52 `one_week` ticks (EP findings §2.4, `advanceMultiTick`). First-visible latency is therefore counted in TICKS the DM can choose to see, and "a one-month advance" is four ticks.

**The planting line** (CHAIR ruling under requirement 16, answering WC-1's Q-5 as a rule rather than a question): a mechanism whose verdict arrives a war or a generation later (comradeship, veterans, diaspora bonds, introduction attempts, release standing, INT-5, WF-7's temple standing, the EP year anchor) declares as its FAST-LAYER SIGN a PLANTING LINE at the moment of cause — a dossier or Herald sentence that names the cause in the record's voice ("the Emberford company served beside Thornhold's through the winter siege") and nothing about the future. Under the Evidence Law it is complete (who, what, where) and not a hint; the slow verdict, when it comes, names the planting as its cause (requirement 20). The planting line is the FIRST-VISIBLE entry those waves carry in §8's brackets.

No flag writes the record whole, and each family's dark-after-lit is its own (PL §10). TWO FACTS on every protection occasion (OD-PL-53): the ANSWER (`relief_sent · relief_kept`) carries the patch; the OUTCOME (`protection_kept · protection_failed`) moves the hold's `P` and patches nothing; the receipt says which ("sent, but too few"); grip moves by resentment, never by trust (PL §4.9). `reliefAnswerOf` is PL's one pure, draw-free relief decider (PL §4.3), shared with MN-4 and called by THE PASSAGE's incursion leaf at FIRST SIGHT, its answer the incursion card's default during the pause (§E.2). §E.3's "`FEALTY_ROW_FAMILIES`" is the chair's label for PL §8's table; PL mints no such identifier. `reliefAnswerOf`, `fealty_sworn` and `fealty_renewed` are FREE (0 src · 0 tests, `cure/D-FOLD-r3-probe/tokens-r3.out`) |
| ↳ `fealty_*` — THE DEN'S TWO CLOSINGS, THE PEACE AND THE BANNER (PL, ST, BD) [AMENDED 2026-10-18 (fp-final r4; SEAMS-r3-5 (MAJOR, the fold's half); `briefs/RULINGS-ROUND4.md` A.1, A.6, A.7 and §E (PL-r4-S1); `cure/D-PL-r4.md` §5, `cure/D-ST-r4.md` and `cure/L-BD.md` §4, routed to the fold)] | the r3 row's families and two facts; ST's den occasion wrote only `protection_failed`, so with obligations dark the hold's `P` could only fall (SEAMS-r3-5); no row for a member's land exporting trouble; no row for riders lent to the banner | (1) THE DEN'S OCCASION CLOSES BOTH WAYS (A.6): `den_unanswered` (ST §6.6) closes ONCE under its one episode `den_unanswered:<siteId>:<tick>` — `protection_kept` when the den is cleared within its one-season clock, by the liege's answer or by anyone; `protection_failed` when the clock ends unanswered — and BOTH closings ride the REQUESTED family's flags (`siteFatesEnabled` ∧ `polityEnabled`), never `polityObligationsEnabled`, so the two outcomes are symmetric under every legal flag set; it never fires for a `keptSecret` den (A.16). (2) THE INCURSION'S OUTCOME IS ITS HARM (§E, PL-r4-S1): `protection_kept` when the member suffered no incursion harm, `protection_failed` when it did, whatever the answer was; the ANSWER (`relief_sent · relief_kept`) moves trust, and it is MINTED at the week's resolution from the line as it stands, never at first sight (A.2; PL §4.3, OD-PL-54 · OD-PL-55). (3) THE PEACE (BD §10.2; PL §4.8, OD-PL-57): OBLIGATION gains `peace_unkept` — a member's land exporting a body's trouble — the SAME word and meaning as BD's incident `peace_unkept` (the neighbour's resentment patch, a `bodyKind` field), an aligned pair of records of one fact family (the pair's incident · the fealty row); it is the MEMBER's default, never the liege's. (4) THE BANNER (§3.3.15): OBLIGATION's protection rows record whether the kingdom's net RODE for a levied member (riders placed = `protection_kept`); a lender's yearly credit is ONE `service_answered` row on PL's own episode `riders_lent:<rootId>:<yearStartTick>` (no registered prefix: PL mints it), and `riders_lent` is also PL's §15.9 incident word for the peacetime lend's wear; a sweep ordered unasked is precedence friction, a resentment patch. (5) The r3 row's citation "PL §4.9" now resolves to PL's section WHAT MOVES THE OATH (round 4). All FREE (`cure/D-FOLD-r4-probe/census-r4.out`: `peace_unkept`, `riders_lent`, `huntOrderOf`, `den_unanswered`, `protection_kept`, `protection_failed` 0 · 0 in `src` and `tests`) |
| ↳ `fealty_*` — THE DEN'S CLOSINGS AS THE SITES WROTE THEM; `peace_unkept` IS A REQUESTED ROW (PL, ST, BD) [AMENDED 2026-10-19 (fp-final r5; the two FAILS of `skeptic/FOLD-r4-verify.md` — SEAMS-r3-5's fold half (MAJOR, drift from the owning volume) and the D-PL-r4 route mis-applied (ROUTED-UNAPPLIED); `briefs/RULINGS-ROUND5.md` B.22 (the fold's mirror drift); `cure/D-PL-r5.md` §5, routed to the fold; ST §6.6 and OD-ST-46, PL §4.8 (1) and §8 as they stand)] | the r4 row's (1) "`protection_failed` when the clock ends unanswered" and its (3) "OBLIGATION gains `peace_unkept`" | THE OWNING VOLUMES' WORDS, of record (§3.4's rule). (1) THE DEN'S OCCASION (ST §6.6, OD-ST-46): `den_unanswered` closes ONCE under `den_unanswered:<siteId>:<tick>` — `protection_kept` when the den is CLEARED within its one-season clock, by the liege's answer or by anyone, AND the site then stands unheld or held by a holder the member does not hold hostile (a hostile force that clears a den and keeps it leaves a den, so the occasion runs on — THE SITES' round-5 REFINED); `protection_failed` when the clock EXPIRES with the den still holding, WHETHER the liege came and was repelled or never came (the receipt says which: "Kell's lord came, but the robbers held the keep"); and NO ROW when the den ends or leaves within the clock by its own course (no protection was tested). The r4 row's "when the clock ends unanswered" is struck: it named only the never-came case. Both closings stay REQUESTED rows under `siteFatesEnabled` ∧ `polityEnabled`. (2) `peace_unkept` IS A REQUESTED ROW (PL §4.8 (1): "It is the REQUESTED family's (§8)"; PL §8's REQUESTED row lists "THE BODIES' `peace_unkept` and harbouring (`body:`)"): BD requests it through `requestFealtyRow` (episode `body:`), and it is written under the requester's own flag ∧ `polityEnabled` — BD's `bodySpillEnabled`, whose liege face also needs `polityObligationsEnabled` by BD's own gate — with NO patch and never read by `P` (OD-PL-57); it is an obligation by MEANING only. The r4 row's "OBLIGATION gains `peace_unkept`" reads "the REQUESTED family gains `peace_unkept`", beside THE SITES' two den closings and THE BODIES' harbouring row, as D-PL-r4's route said. The r4 row's (2), (4) and (5) stand (ST §6.6, OD-ST-46, round 5; PL §4.8, §8, OD-PL-52 · OD-PL-57; §3.4 ↳ r4) |

**§3.2 addendum — the REINFORCE binding (CHAIR ruling, vetoable; RE-RULED after skeptic S4 M1/M2/m8).** The greyed realm verb `REINFORCE` belongs to W-CONVERGENCE (`convergence.js`; its dial `allyId` "the ally under siege"; it lands with the convergence spatial-column seam, `realmManifest.js:471-487`), and the reactive `reinforce` is the belief-priced relief column to a besieged SETTLEMENT. WC credits an ARMY. So: WC-6 (the spatial arm), not WC-4, BINDS the verb — re-targeting its dial to the army, declaring the phantom policy `real-only` (L10(c)), the authority class "directed dispatch" (a DM order), the `reinforce_deferred` veto-code move (L10(e)), and naming W-CONVERGENCE as the program whose seam is taken; the own-army flow `reinforcement.js` (live, unflagged where `warLayerEnabled`; it moves strength and `supplyIntegrity`, not headcount) stays OUTSIDE the seam as a READ; the existing `resupply` seal (§12.2 row 5, WY-8a's `ORDER_RESUPPLY`) is a different seal and stands. The fold's open question at the compiled fold's WC-4 row is answered by this row; Edit Mode's visibility (U16) is untouched.

## §6 THE FELT CORE — what each family keeps as its felt chain, and what is recorded with a slot (the chair's RECOMMENDATION under FP-40 chain-first; the re-sequencing itself is OD-FC-19)

Under the owner's standing words ("no just build it all shipped lit" 09-23; "focus primarily on the ui and the prose first and then everything else after" 10-02; FP-40 CHAIN-FIRST), nothing below retires a wave. Every wave not in a felt chain keeps its charter and gains a SLOT (the never-deferred law): its chain's name. The INVENTORY of coherence chains, named C0..C8 (the register, §5), each ending on an experience read (requirement 19). Under FP-40 only the NEXT chain is fixed — C0 first, C1 second (FP-40's own chain 2, the statecraft band) — and every later one is CHOSEN at the previous chain's read, never written ahead; the order below is the default a read may overturn.

## §8 WHAT WAS AMENDED WHERE — every volume, in place, by eight lanes (2026-10-16, 16:5x–18:5x EDT; one session-limit death at ~17:06 and a resume at 18:28, hazard 93)

**Method.** Each lane copied the MASTER copy (the kit's `volumes-master/`; the ledger `volumes/` copy for CN/LG/PG/MF; the ledger file itself for EC) over the ledger `docs/` destination and then applied every refinement of its findings files, every §3 row naming its lines, the §5 register at first use, the status-header bracket, and a FIRST-VISIBLE · TIER · DEGRADED ARM line on every wave charter — by scripted exact-anchor insertion, each anchor asserted once in the base, with an executed INSERTIONS-ONLY proof (remove every inserted chunk → the base byte for byte). No law, ruling, owner quotation, sha or test name was altered. The lanes' worklogs, scripts and applied-maps are in the kit's `amend/`; the items the lanes raised for a ruling, and the chair's ruling on each, are in `amend/LANE-RAISED.md` (A1–A3, B1–B6, C1–C2, D1–D3, E1–E5, F-WY1–2, F-HB1–7, F-EP1–4, G1–G2, H1–H8) — those rulings are applied in the volumes as further brackets where they bear.

**The owner-row brackets.** After the owner's 18:4x word (§7), every `[OWNER ROW OD-FC-n — …]` head in every volume was rewritten by one scripted pass to `[RULED BY DELEGATION 2026-10-16 (OD-FC-n — …]` (92 in the first fourteen files, 60 more across the rest; residual zero).

**The proof of harmlessness.** Master carries tests that READ these volumes (`tests/lint/infoConvergenceContract.walker.test.js` parses INFORMATION; `tradeConvergenceContract` PIN-6 parses TRADE §3; `tests/docs/*` and the `tests/lint` walkers scan `docs/**`; `ledger-citations`, `enforcement-claims`, the tuning register). The amended set was copied into a detached master worktree at `00b648c9d` and the doc-scanning set run twice: dry run one (the first fourteen files): 40 files, 1,629 tests, green; dry run two (every file, `tests/docs` + `tests/lint` whole + the doc-reading domain and copy tests): **241 files, 4,522 tests, exit 0** (CONFIRMED; the log is the kit's `amend/docs-dryrun-2.log`). dry run three (after requirement 24's fourteen insertions): **238 files, 4,485 tests, exit 0** (`amend/docs-dryrun-3.log`). dry run four (after every skeptic cure, the final text): **238 files, 4,485 tests, exit 0** (`amend/docs-dryrun-4.log`); the scratch worktree was reset afterwards (S4 m6). None of the four is the authoritative gate; the gate runs when the volumes reach master in a docs commit.

## §9 THE ADVERSARIAL REVIEW — five skeptic seats over the fold and every amended volume (2026-10-16, 19:0x–19:3x EDT); every finding disposed under the owner's "whatever is emergent" word (§7)

**Method.** Five Opus seats, each with `SKEPTIC-BRIEF.md` (the attack surfaces in order: a ruling against a landed law or owner word; a ruling that cannot be built at `00b648c9d`; a bracket that alters a law; a false bracket; a wrong §2 cell; internal contradiction; a mis-gated decision; a dropped reader finding; vacuity). Reports: the kit's `skeptic/S1.md` (fold §1–§3.1/§3.3 + SP/CW/GR/WF), `S2.md` (§3.2/§4/§5/§6 + IN/ES/TR/EC), `S3.md` (OD-FC-1..34 + POP/INT/WY/HB/EP), `S4.md` (OD-FC-35..40, the REINFORCE addendum + WC/ARCH/CN/LG/PG/MF), `S5.md` (requirement 24 and the engagement registry). **Totals: 12 BLOCKING · 55 MAJOR · 52 MINOR before S5.** Every amended volume passed every seat's insertion-only check; about 160 bracket addresses were re-read and all but seven held (the seven are cured below). The defects were in the RULINGS, not the amendments — which is what the review was for.

**Dispositions.** CURED = the fold and the volumes now carry the correction (where: §n or the volume's bracket). ACCEPTED-NOTE = true, recorded, no text change needed beyond the note. REJECTED = the finding is wrong, with the reason.
